\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{k}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)\underline{W_{gh}W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}u_{L}(gh,k)^{\dagger}}

\displaystyle\underline{W_{ghk}W_{hk}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{L}(g,hk)}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{ghk})(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}}}\right)\text{\sout{$(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}$}}

\displaystyle\>\text{\sout{$K^{R}_{g}$}}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u_{R}(h,k)^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{hk}^{R}}}\right)(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}.

We will now use the fact that the
u_{L}(g,hk)
commutes with everything that has support only on the right. Combining this with lemma
5.8
gives

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{h}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}u_{L}(g,h)^{\dagger}

(6.10)

\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{k}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{gh}W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}u_{L}(gh,k)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle W_{ghk}W_{hk}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{ghk})(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}}}\right)\underline{u_{L}(g,hk)}

\displaystyle\>\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u_{R}(h,k)^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{hk}^{R}}}\right)(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{h}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}u_{L}(g,h)^{\dagger}

(6.11)

\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{k}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{gh}W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}u_{L}(gh,k)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{ghk}W_{hk}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{ghk})(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\underline{u_{R}(g,hk)^{\dagger}}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u_{R}(h,k)^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{hk}^{R}}}\right)(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}.

Doing the same with
u_{L}(gh,k)^{\dagger}
now gives

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{h}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}u_{L}(g,h)^{\dagger}

(6.12)

\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{k}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}W_{k}\text{\sout{$W_{ghk}^{\dagger}W_{ghk}$}}W_{hk}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{ghk})(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\underline{u_{R}(gh,k)}u_{R}(g,hk)^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u_{R}(h,k)^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{hk}^{R}}}\right)(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}.

When also applying this to
u_{L}(g,h)^{\dagger}
one gets

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{h}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}

(6.13)

\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{k}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{ghk})(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}\underline{u_{R}(g,h)}u_{R}(gh,k)u_{R}(g,hk)^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u_{R}(h,k)^{\dagger}}}\right)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{hk}^{R}}}\right)(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}

Filling in the equation for the 3-cochain (equation (
6.5
)) now gives

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underline{C^{\prime}(g,h,k)}K_{g}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{h}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}

(6.14)

\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{k}^{R}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{gh}W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}\text{\sout{$W_{g}^{\dagger}$}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\underline{\mathbbm{1}}\text{\sout{$\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)$}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{ghk})(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{g}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(K_{hk}^{R}}}\right)(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}}}\right)(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}.

Fully writing out the adjoints now gives:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C^{\prime}(g,h,k)K_{g}^{R}W_{g}K_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}

(6.15)

\displaystyle K_{gh}^{R}W_{gh}K_{k}^{R}W_{k}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}(K_{ghk}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{ghk}W_{k}^{\dagger}W_{gh}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle W_{gh}W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}W_{hk}W_{ghk}^{\dagger}K^{R}_{ghk}W_{ghk}W_{hk}^{\dagger}(K^{R}_{hk})^{\dagger}W_{hk}W_{k}^{\dagger}W_{gh}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}W_{g}W_{h}W_{k}W_{hk}^{\dagger}K_{hk}^{R}W_{hk}W_{k}^{\dagger}(K^{R}_{k})^{\dagger}W_{h}^{\dagger}(K^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}(K^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}C^{\prime}(g,h,k)

(6.16)

concluding the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.3
.

The function
C
as defined in lemma
6.1
is a 2-cochain.

Proof.

Take the 3-cochain
C^{\prime}
introduced in the last lemma. It is clear that this 3-cochain is invariant under the substitution

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\rightarrow v^{\dagger}W_{g}v

\displaystyle u_{R}(g,h)

\displaystyle\rightarrow v^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)v.

(6.17)

If we now prove that it is also invariant under the substitution in equation
6.6
, we have proved our result because using equation (
6.1
) we then get that

\text{\sout{$C^{\prime}(g,h,k)$}}=C(h,k)C(gh,k)^{-1}C(g,hk)C(g,h)^{-1}\text{\sout{$C^{\prime}(g,h,k)$}}

(6.18)

proving the result. The invariance of the three cochain under this substitution is proven in lemma
6.2
.
∎

Our translation index is now defined as

Definition 6.4
.

Let
C:G^{2}\rightarrow U(1)
be the 2-cochain defined in
6.1
. Take
\phi:G^{2}\rightarrow\mathbb{T}
such that
C(g,h)=\exp(i\phi(g,h))
then we define the index as

\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0},\Theta,\omega,\omega_{0})\vcentcolon=\expectationvalue{\phi}\in H^{2}(G,\mathbb{T})

(6.19)

and (as advertised) it is only a function of the automorphisms (and the product state) not on the choice of the GNS triple of
\omega_{0}
or on the choice of phases in
W_{g},u_{L}(g,h),u_{R}(g,h),v,K_{g}^{L}
and
K_{g}^{R}
.
17
17

17

Even though we explicitly wrote down the on-site group action
U
, this index is only dependent on
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U}}\right)
.

Proof.

The construction is invariant under the choice of GNS triple since this simply amounts to an adjoint action by some unitary on every operator. Now we will show that it is invariant under the choice of phases of our operators. Clearly the 2-cochain
C
is invariant under

\displaystyle u_{L}(g,h)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\alpha(g)\alpha(g)\alpha(gh)^{-1}\beta(g,h)^{-1}u_{L}(g,h)

\displaystyle u_{R}(g,h)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\beta(g,h)u_{R}(g,h)

(6.20)

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\alpha(g)W_{g}

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\rightarrow\gamma v.

Under the transformation

\displaystyle K_{g}^{L}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\delta(g)^{-1}K_{g}^{L}

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\delta(g)K_{g}^{R}

(6.21)

we get
C(g,h)\rightarrow\delta(g)\delta(h)\delta(gh)^{-1}C(g,h)
which is still in the same equivalence class concluding the proof.
∎

6.2
The
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued index

\theta
\eta^{L}_{g}
\eta^{R}_{g}
\Theta
\bullet
{{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\scalebox{0.5}{-}1,\scalebox{0.5}{-}1}}\right)}}

Figure 3
:
This figure indicates the support area of the different automorphisms when there are two translation directions. The angle
\theta
still has to be smaller then or equal to what was indicated here so that the
\Theta
and the
\eta_{g}
commute but now we must be able to do both a horizontal and a vertical widening of the support of
\eta_{g}
. Also the coordinate
(-1,-1)
which will be of particular interest is indicated.

Lemma 6.5
.

There exists an
\alpha:G\rightarrow U(1)
satisfying

\displaystyle v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\alpha(g)w^{\dagger}K_{g}^{R}w

where

\displaystyle h_{g}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(U_{(-1,-1)}(g)).

(6.22)

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}K_{g}^{R}w}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\tau\circ(\beta_{g}^{RU})^{-1}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

(6.23)

Now we will use the fact that because of translation invariance of the group action, we have that
\tau^{-1}\circ\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\tau\circ(\beta_{g}^{RU})^{-1}=\beta_{g}^{RL_{-1}}
with
L_{j}=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}|y=j\}
. This leads to

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\underline{\tau^{-1}}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu

(6.24)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\underline{v^{\dagger}}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

Inserting
b^{R}_{g}vv^{\dagger}(b^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}
now gives

=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}\underline{b^{R}_{g}vv^{\dagger}(b^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

(6.25)

Using the inverse of equation (
5.26
) we now get

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b^{R}_{g}v}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\underline{\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\text{\sout{$(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu$}}}\circ\text{\sout{$\nu^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}$}}\circ\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu

(6.26)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}v}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

Now we will use the fact that
\nu^{-1}\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ\nu=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U_{(-1,-1)}(g)}}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}
to obtain

=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}v}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U_{(-1,-1)}(g)}}\right)\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

(6.27)

Since (as you can check in figure
3
)
\tau^{-1}(\eta_{g}^{R}(\tau(U_{(-1,-1)}(g))))=U_{(-1,-1)}(g)
this gives

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}v}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U_{(-1,-1)}(g)}}\right)\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu

(6.28)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

Now using again that
\tau\circ\beta_{g}^{R,L_{-1}}=\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\tau\circ(\beta_{g}^{RU})^{-1}
we get:

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\tau\circ(\beta_{g}^{RU})^{-1}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu

(6.29)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g}^{R}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\nu.

After again using (
5.26
) we obtain:

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta.

(6.30)

By the irreducibility of
\pi_{0}
, this implies that there indeed exists such an
\alpha
.
∎

We have that
h_{g}\in\textrm{IAC}_{L}(\theta,\alpha_{0},\Theta)
. On top of that, we also have that

Lemma 6.6
.

For all
g,h\in G
we have that

\displaystyle[h_{g},W_{h}]

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle[h_{g},b_{h}^{L}]

\displaystyle=0.

(6.31)

Proof.

We have that (see figure
3
)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{h}}}\right)(h_{g})

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{h}\circ\beta_{h}^{U}(U_{(-1,-1)}(g))=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(U_{(-1,-1)}(g))=h_{g}

(6.32)

concluding the proof of the first item. The second proof is analogous.
∎

Finally, before we can show that the
\alpha
is a
U(1)
representation we need to prove an analog of the equation in the 2-cochain lemma
6.1
:

Lemma 6.7
.

The equality

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b_{g}^{R}W_{g}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)w}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

(6.33)

holds.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of
6.1
. We now show that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b_{g}W_{g}b_{h}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{L}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}(g,h)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)\otimes u_{R}(g,h)w}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

(6.34)

holds. This part is completely the same. Afterwards we have to prove that:
b_{g}W_{g}b_{h}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}
, is split. This is a straightforward calculation.
∎

We now need to prove the analogue of
6.2
.

Lemma 6.8
.

The two-cochain as defined through

K_{g}^{R}W_{g}K_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)=C(g,h)v^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)v.

(6.35)

is invariant under the substitution

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}

\displaystyle\mapsto v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}W_{g}w

\displaystyle u_{R}(g,h)

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)w.

(6.36)

Proof.

To show this, notice that we have by construction and by lemma
6.7
that

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b_{g}W_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}W_{g}w}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

(6.37)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b_{g}^{R}W_{g}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)w}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}.

(6.38)

This shows that there exists a
\beta:G\rightarrow U(1)
and a
\gamma:G\times G\rightarrow U(1)
such that

\displaystyle w^{\dagger}W_{g}w

\displaystyle=\beta(g)b_{g}^{L}b_{g}^{R}W_{g}

\displaystyle w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)w

\displaystyle=\gamma(g,h)b_{g}^{R}W_{g}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h).

(6.39)

Since the equation is invariant under the substitution with these two phases all that is left to do is to show that equation (
6.35
) is invariant under the substitution:

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}

\displaystyle\mapsto v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}=v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vK_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}h_{g}

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\mapsto b_{g}^{L}b_{g}^{R}W_{g}

(6.40)

u_{R}(g,h)\mapsto b_{g}^{R}W_{g}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h).

(6.41)

To show this first notice that by using the fact that
h_{g}\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\theta,\alpha_{0},\Theta)^{\prime}
and lemma
6.6
we get that

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}W_{g}K_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}

(6.42)

\displaystyle\mapsto v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}v\underline{h_{g}}K_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}b_{g}^{L}b_{g}^{R}W_{g}v^{\dagger}b_{h}^{R}v\underline{h_{h}}K_{h}^{R}(b_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}b_{h}^{L}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{L}b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}(v^{\dagger}b_{gh}^{R}v\underline{h_{gh}}K_{gh}^{R}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\underline{h_{g}h_{h}h_{gh}^{\dagger}}v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vK_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}b_{g}^{L}b_{g}^{R}W_{g}v^{\dagger}b_{h}^{R}vK_{h}^{R}(b_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}b_{h}^{L}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{L}b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}(v^{\dagger}b_{gh}^{R}vK_{gh}^{R}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger})^{\dagger}

(all the
h-
operators can be put in front). Since
h_{g}
is an honest representation we get that the
h-
operators cancel out. This leaves us with checking that equation (
6.35
) is invariant under

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}

\displaystyle\mapsto v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vK_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\mapsto b_{g}^{L}b_{g}^{R}W_{g}

(6.43)

u_{R}(g,h)\mapsto b_{g}^{R}W_{g}b_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h).

Using the fact that

\displaystyle(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)

\displaystyle=(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}u_{L}(g,h)^{\dagger}=u_{L}(g,h)^{\dagger}(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}=W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)(b_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}

(6.44)

we see that we can prove that our equation is invariant under the substitution using lemma
7.3
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Using this we can show that:

Lemma 6.9
.

The
\alpha
defined previously is a
U(1)
-representation.

Proof.

Take the definition of the 2-cochain (lemma
6.1
). This equation is invariant under the substitution

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}K_{g}^{R}w

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}W_{g}w

\displaystyle u_{R}(g,h)

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)w.

(6.45)

If we now also show that it is invariant under the substitution

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}

\displaystyle\mapsto v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g}^{R}(b_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle W_{g}

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}W_{g}w

\displaystyle u_{R}(g,h)

\displaystyle\mapsto w^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)w

(6.46)

then we get using the definition of
\alpha
that

\text{\sout{$C(g,h)$}}=\text{\sout{$C(g,h)$}}\frac{\alpha(g)\alpha(h)}{\alpha(gh)}

(6.47)

which would conclude the proof. This invariance is proven in lemma
6.8
.
∎

Definition 6.10
.

Let
\alpha
be the
U(1)
-representation defined in lemma
6.5
. Take
\phi\in\hom(G,\mathbb{T})
such that
\alpha(g)=\exp(i\phi(g))
. We define the 2 translation index as

\textrm{Index}_{2\>\text{trans}}^{\mathcal{A},U}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0},\Theta,\omega,\omega_{0})\vcentcolon=\phi\in H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})

(6.48)

and (as advertised) it is only a function of the automorphisms (and the product state) not of the choice of the GNS triple of
\omega_{0}
or the choice of phases in
W_{g},u_{L}(g,h),u_{R}(g,h),v,w,K^{L}_{g},K^{R}_{g},b^{L}_{g}
and
b^{R}_{g}
.
18
18

18

It is however explicitly dependent on the on-site group action
U
, and not only
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(U}}\right)
.

Proof.

The construction is invariant under the choice of GNS triple since this simply amounts to an adjoint action by some unitary on every operator. The proof that it is independent of the choice of phases is just as trivial.
∎

One can also define this index starting from operators acting on the left. As the following lemma shows this gives us the opposite index (the complex conjugate of the
U(1)
-representation):

Lemma 6.11
.

Define
\tilde{\alpha}(g)
such that

v^{\dagger}b_{g}^{L}vh_{g}^{\dagger}K_{g}^{L}(b_{g}^{L})^{\dagger}=\tilde{\alpha}(g)w^{\dagger}K_{g}^{L}w

(6.49)

then
\alpha(g)\tilde{\alpha}(g)=1
.

Proof.

By multiplying the left hand side of equation (
6.49
) with the left hand side of equation (
6.22
) we obtain

v^{\dagger}b_{g}vK_{g}b_{g}^{\dagger}=v^{\dagger}b_{g}vv^{\dagger}W_{g}vW_{g}^{\dagger}b_{g}^{\dagger}=w^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}W_{g}vW_{g}^{\dagger}w=w^{\dagger}K_{g}w

(6.50)

whereas by multiplying the right-hand sides of equations (
6.49
) and (
6.22
) we would obtain
\alpha(g)\tilde{\alpha}(g)w^{\dagger}K_{g}w
. These two results can only be consistent if the lemma holds.
∎

7
The indices are well-defined

In this section, we will show that the index from definition
6.4
and the index from definition
6.10
are only dependent on
\omega
and not on the choices of our automorphisms nor on
\omega_{0}
. On this last item, we remark:

Remark 7.1
.

A product state can have a non-trivial
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
index and the index is not defined relative to
\omega_{0}
so in particular

\textrm{Index}_{2\>\text{trans}}^{\mathcal{A},U}(\theta,\beta_{g}^{U},\textrm{Id},\textrm{Id},\textrm{Id},\omega_{0},\omega_{0})

(7.1)

can still be non-trivial. In fact in this case we get
\alpha(g)=\omega_{0}(U_{(-1,-1)}(g))
. To show this, notice that we can take
b_{g}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}}
and we can choose
K_{g}^{R}
so that it leaves the cyclic vector of
\omega_{0}
,
\Omega_{0}
invariant. Applying the definition of the index on this cyclic vector then gives us
h_{g}\Omega_{0}=\alpha(g)\Omega_{0}
. This obviously implies that
\bra{\Omega_0}\ket{h_g\Omega_0}=\alpha(g)\bra{\Omega_0}\ket{\Omega_0}
giving us indeed the advertised equation.

We now show independence on
\alpha
and the choice of its decomposition.

Lemma 7.2
.

Take
\omega_{01},\omega_{02}\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
product states and let
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}}}\right)
be such that
\omega_{01}\circ\alpha_{1}=\omega_{02}\circ\alpha_{2}=\omega
. Let
V_{11},V_{12}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A})
,
\alpha_{L/R,1},\alpha_{L/R,2}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{L/R}}}\right)
and
\Theta_{1},\Theta_{2}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}}}}\right)
be such that
\alpha_{1}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{11}}}\right)\circ\alpha_{01}\circ\Theta_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{12}}}\right)\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\Theta_{2}
with
\alpha_{0,i}=\alpha_{L,i}\otimes\alpha_{R,i}
, then

\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0,1},\Theta_{1},\omega,\omega_{01})=\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0,2},\Theta_{2},\omega,\omega_{02}).

(7.2)

Additionally, when
\omega
satisfies assumption
3.5
,

\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}_{\text{2 trans}}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0,1},\Theta_{1},\omega,\omega_{01})=\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}_{\text{2 trans}}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0,2},\Theta_{2},\omega,\omega_{02}).

(7.3)

Proof.

We will first prove the result in the case that
\omega_{0}=\omega_{01}=\omega_{02}
and then generalize this result. Since
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\omega_{0}
there exists a
\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{0})
such that
\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}^{-1}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{x}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}
. Now define
x\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{0})
to be
x\vcentcolon=\pi_{0}(V_{12}^{\dagger})\tilde{x}\pi_{0}(V_{11})
then

\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\Theta_{2}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(x}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{01}\circ\Theta_{1}.

(7.4)

Now take
W_{g,1},u_{R,1}(g,h),v
and
K^{R}_{g,1}
to be the operators belonging to the first choice (with arbitrary phases). When,
\omega
satisfies assumption
3.5
, also take
b^{R}_{g,1}
and
w
, the additional operators belonging to the first choice.
We have (see
[
10
]
lemma 2.11)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(xW_{g,1}x^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{02}^{-1},

(7.5)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(xu_{R,1}(g,h)x^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\eta_{h}^{R}\circ\beta_{h}^{RU}\circ(\beta_{gh}^{RU})^{-1}\circ(\eta_{gh}^{R})^{-1}\circ\alpha_{02}^{-1}.

Through similar arguments we get

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(xvx^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\tau\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{02}^{-1}

(7.6)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(xK^{R}_{g,1}x^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\tau^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\beta_{g}^{RU}\circ\tau\circ(\beta_{g}^{RU})^{-1}\circ(\eta^{R}_{g})^{-1}\circ\alpha_{02}^{-1}

and

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(xwx^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\Theta_{2}\circ\nu\circ\Theta_{2}^{-1}\circ\alpha_{02}^{-1}

(7.7)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(xb^{R}_{g,1}x^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{02}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\nu\circ(\eta_{g}^{R})^{-1}\circ\alpha_{02}^{-1}

in the case where
\omega
satisfies assumption
3.5
. This shows that
xW_{g,1}x^{\dagger}
,
xu_{R,1}(g,h)x^{\dagger}
,
xvx^{\dagger}
,
xK^{R}_{g,1}x^{\dagger}
,
xwx^{\dagger}
and
xb^{R}_{g,1}
are operators belonging to the second choice. Since our index is invariant under this substitution this concludes the proof when
\omega_{0}=\omega_{01}=\omega_{02}
. Now suppose that
\omega_{01}\neq\omega_{02}
. Since they are both product states there exists a
\gamma\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}}}\right)
satisfying
\omega_{02}=\omega_{01}\circ\gamma
that is of the form
\gamma=\gamma^{L}\otimes\gamma^{R}
. We now have

\textrm{Index}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0,2},\Theta_{2},\omega,\omega_{02})=\textrm{Index}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\alpha_{0,2},\Theta_{2},\omega,\omega_{01}\circ\gamma)=\textrm{Index}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g},\eta_{g},\gamma\circ\alpha_{0,2},\Theta_{2},\omega,\omega_{01})

(7.8)

and similarly for the second index.
∎

We now show that the
H^{2}(G,\mathbb{T})
and
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued indices are invariant under some transformation parametrized by the group
\textrm{IAC}_{R}^{W}
:

Lemma 7.3
.

Take
\omega_{0},\theta,\alpha_{0},\Theta
and
\eta_{g}
as usual, take
v,W_{g,1},u_{\sigma,1}(g,h)
and
K_{g,1}^{\sigma}
the operators corresponding to these automorphisms and take
\delta^{\sigma}_{g}\in\textrm{IAC}_{\sigma}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
(for all
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
and all
g\in G
). Define

\displaystyle W_{g,2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\delta_{g}W_{g,1}

\displaystyle u_{\sigma,2}(g,h)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\delta_{g}^{\sigma}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{\sigma}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{\sigma,1}(g,h)(\delta_{gh}^{\sigma})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle K^{\sigma}_{g,2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{\sigma}vK_{g,1}^{\sigma}(\delta_{g}^{\sigma})^{\dagger}

(7.9)

then equation (
6.1
), defining the index of
v,W_{g,1},u_{\sigma,1}(g,h)
and
K_{g,1}^{\sigma}
, is still valid when we replace these operators by
v,W_{g,2},u_{\sigma,2}(g,h)
and
K_{g,2}^{\sigma}
respectively. The two cochain also remains unchanged.

Lemma 7.4
.

Additionally, on top of the assumptions and definitions of lemma
7.3
, let
\omega
satisfy assumption
3.5
and take
w
and
b_{g,1}^{\sigma}
the additional operators belonging to the automorphisms. Define
b_{g,2}^{\sigma}\vcentcolon=w^{\dagger}\delta^{\sigma}_{g}wb_{g,1}^{\sigma}(\delta^{\sigma}_{g})^{\dagger}
then equation
6.22
, defining the index of
v,w,W_{g,1},u_{\sigma,1}(g,h),K_{g,1}^{\sigma},h_{g}
and
b_{g,1}^{\sigma}
is still valid when we replace these operators by
v,w,W_{g,2},u_{\sigma,2}(g,h),h_{g},K_{g,2}^{\sigma}
and
b_{g,2}^{\sigma}
. The one cochain also remains unchanged.

Proof.

We start by proving lemma
7.3
. First, we will prove that we still have that

u_{L,2}(g,h)u_{R,2}(g,h)=W_{gh,2}W_{h,2}^{\dagger}W_{g,2}^{\dagger}.

(7.10)

To do this first insert the definition to obtain

\displaystyle u_{L,2}(g,h)u_{R,2}(g,h)=\delta_{g}^{L}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{L}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{L,1}(g,h)(\delta_{gh}^{L})^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{R,1}(g,h)(\delta_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}.

(7.11)

Since by lemma
5.8
,
W_{g,1}\delta_{g}^{\sigma}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}\in\textrm{IAC}_{\sigma}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
(for
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
and
g\in G
) we get by repetitive use of equation (
5.9
) that

\displaystyle=\delta_{g}^{L}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{L}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}\underline{\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}}u_{L,1}(g,h)(\delta_{gh}^{L})^{\dagger}u_{R,1}(g,h)(\delta_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}

(7.12)

\displaystyle=\delta_{g}^{L}\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{L}\text{\sout{$W_{g,1}^{\dagger}W_{g,1}$}}\delta_{h}^{R}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{L,1}(g,h)u_{R,1}(g,h)\underline{(\delta_{gh}^{L})^{\dagger}}(\delta_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\delta_{g}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}\text{\sout{$W_{g,1}^{\dagger}W_{g,1}$}}W_{h,1}W_{gh,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{gh})^{\dagger}=W_{g,2}W_{h,2}W_{gh,2}^{\dagger}

concluding the proof of equation (
7.10
). Using this we obtain:

\displaystyle K_{g,2}^{R}W_{g,2}K_{h,2}^{R}W_{h,2}W_{gh,2}^{\dagger}(K_{gh,2}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{gh,2}W_{h,2}^{\dagger}W_{g,2}^{\dagger}u_{R,2}(g,h)=K_{g,2}^{R}W_{g,2}K_{h,2}^{R}W_{h,2}W_{gh,2}^{\dagger}(K_{gh,2}^{R})^{\dagger}u_{L,2}(g,h)^{\dagger}

(7.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{g,2}^{R}W_{g,2}K_{h,2}^{R}W_{h,2}W_{gh,2}^{\dagger}u_{L,2}(g,h)^{\dagger}(K_{gh,2}^{R})^{\dagger}=K_{g,2}^{R}W_{g,2}K_{h,2}^{R}\text{\sout{$W_{h,2}W_{gh,2}^{\dagger}W_{gh,2}W_{h,2}^{\dagger}$}}W_{g,2}^{\dagger}u_{R,2}(g,h)(K_{gh,2}^{R})^{\dagger}.

(7.14)

Filling this in the definition of the 2-cochain gives

\displaystyle C_{2}(g,h)

\displaystyle=K_{g,2}^{R}W_{g,2}K_{h,2}^{R}W_{g,2}^{\dagger}u_{R,2}(g,h)(K_{gh,2}^{R})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}u_{R,2}(g,h)^{\dagger}v

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}\delta_{g}W_{g,1}v^{\dagger}\delta_{h}^{R}vK_{h,1}^{R}(\delta_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{\dagger}

(7.15)

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{R,1}(g,h)\text{\sout{$(\delta_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}\delta_{gh}^{R}$}}(K_{gh,1}^{R})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}\text{\sout{$(\delta_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}vv^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{gh}$}}u_{R,1}(g,h)^{\dagger}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v.

We now insert
(K_{h,1}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}K_{g,1}^{R}W_{g,1}K_{h,1}^{R}=\mathbbm{1}:

\displaystyle C_{2}(g,h)

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}\delta_{g}W_{g,1}v^{\dagger}\delta_{h}^{R}vK_{h,1}^{R}(\delta_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((K_{h,1}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}K_{g,1}^{R}W_{g,1}K_{h,1}^{R}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{R,1}(g,h)(K_{gh,1}^{R})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}u_{R,1}(g,h)^{\dagger}vv^{\dagger}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v.

(7.16)

Filling in the old index now gives:

\displaystyle C_{2}(g,h)

\displaystyle=\underline{C_{1}(g,h)}v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}\delta_{g}W_{g,1}v^{\dagger}\delta_{h}^{R}vK_{h,1}^{R}(\delta_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{\dagger}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}(K_{h,1}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}\underline{\mathbbm{1}}v^{\dagger}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v

Using equation (
5.9
) we now get:

\displaystyle C_{2}(g,h)

\displaystyle=C_{1}(g,h)v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}\text{\sout{$\delta_{g}^{L}$}}W_{g,1}v^{\dagger}\delta_{h}^{R}vK_{h,1}^{R}(\delta_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}\text{\sout{$(\delta_{g}^{L})^{\dagger}$}}

(7.18)

\displaystyle\qquad W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}(K_{h,1}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v

\displaystyle=C_{1}(g,h)v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{\text{\sout{$R$}}}W_{g,1}v^{\dagger}\delta_{h}^{R}v\text{\sout{$K_{h,1}^{R}(\delta_{h}^{R})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}(K_{h,1}^{R})^{\dagger}$}}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{\text{\sout{$R$}}})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v

\displaystyle=C_{1}(g,h)v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}W_{g,1}v^{\dagger}\delta_{h}^{R}vW_{g,1}^{\dagger}(K_{g,1})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v.

Using the fact that by definition
K_{g,1}W_{g,1}=v^{\dagger}W_{g,1}v
we obtain

\displaystyle C_{2}(g,h)

\displaystyle=C_{1}(g,h)v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}\underline{W_{g,1}}\delta_{h}^{R}\underline{W_{g,1}^{\dagger}}W_{g,1}(\delta^{R}_{h})^{\dagger}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}v=C_{1}(g,h)

(7.19)

concluding the proof of lemma
7.3
. We now proceed with the proof of lemma
7.4
. We define the index in function of the new variables:

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(g)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=v^{\dagger}b^{R}_{g,2}vh_{g}K^{R}_{g,2}(b^{R}_{2,g})^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}(K^{R}_{2,g})^{\dagger}w

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}wb_{g,1}^{R}(\delta^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}}}\right)vh_{g}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}wb_{g,1}^{R}(\delta^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}}}\right)^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}(v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger})^{\dagger}w

(7.20)

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}wb_{g,1}^{R}(\delta^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}vh_{g}v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}\text{\sout{$(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}$}}(b_{1,g}^{R})^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}\text{\sout{$(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}ww^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}$}}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}vw.

If we now insert
vv^{\dagger},
we obtain

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(g)

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}wb_{g,1}^{R}v\text{\sout{$v^{\dagger}(\delta^{R}_{g})^{\dagger}v$}}h_{g}\text{\sout{$v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}v$}}K_{g,1}^{R}(b_{1,g}^{R})^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}vw

(7.21)

where the cancelation is due to the fact that
v^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}v\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
whereas
h_{g}\in\textrm{IAC}_{L}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
. Again after inserting
vv^{\dagger}
and
ww^{\dagger}
we now get

\displaystyle\alpha_{2}(g)

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}w\underline{vv^{\dagger}}b_{g,1}^{R}vh_{g}K_{g,1}^{R}(b_{1,g}^{R})^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}(K_{g,1}^{R})^{\dagger}\underline{ww^{\dagger}}v^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}vw

(7.22)

\displaystyle=\text{\sout{$v^{\dagger}w^{\dagger}\delta^{R}_{g}wv$}}\alpha_{1}(g)\text{\sout{$w^{\dagger}v^{\dagger}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}vw$}}=\alpha_{1}(g)

concluding the proof.
∎

We will now show that the index is independent of the choice of
\tilde{\beta}_{g}
and its decomposition.

Lemma 7.5
.

Take
\tilde{\beta}_{g,1},\tilde{\beta}_{g,2}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}}}\right),V_{g,21},V_{g,22}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}),\eta_{g,1}^{L},\eta_{g,2}^{L}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{-1}(L\cap C_{\theta})}}}\right)
and
\eta_{g,1}^{R},\eta_{g,2}^{R}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu(R\cap C_{\theta})}}}\right)
such that there exist
V_{g,21},V_{g,22}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A})
satisfying

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{g,1}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,21}}}\right)\circ\eta_{g,1}\circ\beta^{U}_{g}

\displaystyle\tilde{\beta}_{g,2}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,22}}}\right)\circ\eta_{g,2}\circ\beta^{U}_{g}

and

\displaystyle\omega\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g,1}

\displaystyle=\omega\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g,2}=\omega

(7.23)

then

\textrm{Index}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g,1},\eta_{g,1},\alpha_{0},\Theta,\omega,\omega_{0})=\textrm{Index}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g,2},\eta_{g,2},\alpha_{0},\Theta,\omega,\omega_{0}).

(7.24)

If additionally,
\omega
satisfies assumption
3.5
, then

\textrm{Index}_{\text{2 trans}}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g,1},\eta_{g,1},\alpha_{0},\Theta,\omega,\omega_{0})=\textrm{Index}_{\text{2 trans}}(\theta,\tilde{\beta}_{g,2},\eta_{g,2},\alpha_{0},\Theta,\omega,\omega_{0}).

(7.25)

Proof.

Take
\alpha=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{1}}}\right)\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta
the usual decomposition. Since
\omega_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g,1}\circ(\tilde{\beta}_{g,2})^{-1}=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha
, there exist
\tilde{\delta}_{g}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{0})
such that:
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{\delta}_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha\circ\tilde{\beta}_{g,2}\circ(\tilde{\beta}_{g,1})^{-1}
. Inserting equation (
7.23
) and the decomposition of
\alpha
in this gives

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{\delta}_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{1}}}\right)\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta=\pi_{0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{1}}}\right)\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,21}}}\right)\circ\eta_{g,1}\circ\eta_{g,2}^{-1}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,22}^{\dagger}}}\right).

(7.26)

Putting the
V_{1}
’s to the other side gives:

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{0}(V_{1}^{\dagger})\tilde{\delta}_{g}\pi_{0}(V_{1})}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,21}}}\right)\circ\eta_{g,1}\circ\eta_{g,2}^{-1}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,22}^{\dagger}}}\right).

(7.27)

Doing the same for the
V_{g,21}
and
V_{g,22}
now gives

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(V_{g,21}^{\dagger})\pi_{0}(V_{1}^{\dagger})\tilde{\delta}_{g}\pi_{0}(V_{1})}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{g,22}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{g,2}\circ(\eta_{g,1})^{-1}

(7.28)

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(V_{g,21}^{\dagger})\pi_{0}(V_{1}^{\dagger})\tilde{\delta}_{g}\pi_{0}(V_{1})\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(V_{g,22})}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\eta_{g,2}\circ(\eta_{g,1})^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

(7.29)

Since the last equation is split we have by lemma
5.6
that we can take
\delta_{g}^{L}\in\textrm{Cone}_{\nu^{-1}(L)}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta)
and
\delta_{g}^{R}\in\textrm{Cone}_{\nu(R)}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta)
such that

\delta_{g}^{L}\otimes\delta_{g}^{R}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(V_{g,21}^{\dagger})\pi_{0}(V_{1}^{\dagger})\tilde{\delta}_{g}\pi_{0}(V_{1})\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(V_{g,22}).

(7.30)

Take
W_{g,1},u_{R,1}(g,h)
and
K^{R}_{g,1}
to be the operators belonging to the first choice (with arbitrary phases). Define

\displaystyle W_{g,2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\delta_{g}W_{g,1}

\displaystyle u_{R,2}(g,h)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\delta_{g}^{R}W_{g,1}\delta_{h}^{R}W_{g,1}^{\dagger}u_{R,1}(g,h)(\delta_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle K^{R}_{g,2}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=v^{\dagger}\delta_{g}^{R}vK_{g,1}^{R}(\delta_{g}^{R})^{\dagger}

(7.31)

then by construction
W_{g,2},u_{R,2}(g,h)
and
K^{R}_{g,2}
are operators belonging to the second choice. Now using lemma
7.3
concludes the proof. The proof of the second part is completely analogous, only we now define,
b_{g,2}^{\sigma}\vcentcolon=w^{\dagger}\delta^{\sigma}_{g}wb_{g,1}^{\sigma}(\delta^{\sigma}_{g})^{\dagger}
as well and conclude the proof by using lemma
7.4
.
∎

Lemma 7.6
.

Both indices are independent of the choice of angle
\theta
.

Proof.

Take
0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}<\pi/2
. Take
\alpha\in\textrm{QAut}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}}}\right)
and (for all
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
) take
\eta_{g,1}^{\sigma}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma\cap C_{\theta_{1}})}}}\right)
and
\eta_{g,2}^{\sigma}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma\cap C_{\theta_{2}})}}}\right)
to be operators belonging to
\tilde{\beta}_{g,1}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{g,2}
leaving
\omega
invariant. Since
\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma\cap C_{\theta_{1}})}}}\right)\subset\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma\cap C_{\theta_{2}})}}}\right)
the result now follows from lemma
7.5
.
∎

Due to all these considerations, from here onward, we will write the
H^{2}(G,\mathbb{T})
and
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued indices as
\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}(\omega)
and
\textrm{Index}^{\mathcal{A},U}_{\text{2 trans}}(\omega)
respectively.

8
Examples

In subsections
8.1
and
8.2
we will give a special class of translation invariant states that satisfy assumptions
3.2
and
3.5
respectively. We start our construction by taking a state
\phi\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A}_{L_{0}})
that satisfies assumption
3.3
. This state satisfies the following lemma:

Lemma 8.1
.

This
\phi
satisfies the split property.

Proof.

Take
(\mathcal{H}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{R},\pi_{L}\otimes\pi_{R},\Omega_{L}\otimes\Omega_{R})
a GNS triple of
\phi_{0}
. By construction
(\mathcal{H}_{L}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{R},\pi_{L}\circ\tilde{\alpha}_{L}\otimes\pi_{R}\circ\tilde{\alpha}_{R},\pi_{L}\otimes\pi_{R}(b^{\dagger})\Omega_{L}\otimes\Omega_{R})
is a GNS triple of
\phi
. This concludes the proof that
\phi
satisfies the split property.
∎

For any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, take

\displaystyle L_{j}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}|y=j\}

\displaystyle L_{j}^{n}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}|y=j,x\in[-n,n]\}

(8.1)

\displaystyle L_{<j}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}|y<j\}

\displaystyle L_{>j}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\{(x,y)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}|y>j\}.

(8.2)

We introduce some additional notation, for any automorphism
\alpha\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{L_{0}}}}\right)
we define
\alpha_{(\cdot,j)}\vcentcolon=\tau^{j}\circ\alpha\circ\tau^{-j}
. We will now define the infinite tensor product state.

Definition 8.2
.

Take
\phi\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A}_{L_{0}})
arbitrary. There exists a unique state
\omega
satisfying that for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}
we have that for any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{L_{i}}
and
B\in\mathcal{A}_{L_{i}^{c}}
,
\omega(A\otimes B)=\phi\circ\tau^{-i}(A)\omega(B)
.

Proof.

This condition gives a construction by induction for
\omega(A)
for each
A\in\mathcal{A}_{\text{loc}}
. For general elements we define
\omega(A)\vcentcolon=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\omega(A_{n})
where
A_{n}
is a sequence in
\mathcal{A}_{\text{loc}}
converging in
\mathcal{A}
.
∎

In what follows, we will fix a GNS triple for
\omega_{0}
,
(\mathcal{H}_{0},\pi_{0},\Omega_{0})
that is of the form:
\mathcal{H}_{0}=\bigotimes_{\rho\sigma}\mathcal{H}_{\rho,\sigma}
,
\pi_{0}=\bigotimes_{\rho\sigma}\pi_{\rho,\sigma}
and
\Omega_{0}=\bigotimes_{\rho\sigma}\Omega_{\rho,\sigma}
, where the
\pi_{\rho,\sigma}:\mathcal{A}_{\rho\cap\sigma}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(\mathcal{H}_{\rho,\sigma})
are all irreducible representations for all
\rho\in\{L_{>-1},L_{-1},L_{<-1}\}
and
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
.

Remark 8.3
.

We have (by construction) that
\pi_{L_{-1},L}\circ\alpha_{L}|_{L_{-1}}\otimes\pi_{L_{-1},R}\circ\alpha_{R}|_{L_{-1}}
is a GNS representation of
\phi
.

8.1
Example with non-trivial
H^{2}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued index

By the result of
[
9
]
, lemma
8.1
implies that
\phi
has a well-defined 1d SPT index. The following lemma says that the infinite tensor product state also has a well-defined
H^{2}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued index.

Lemma 8.4
.

Let
\omega
be the infinite tensor product of a state
\phi
that satisfies assumption
3.3
then
\omega
satisfies assumption
3.2
.

Proof.

To show that
\tilde{\beta}_{g}
exists note that we can take
\tilde{\beta}_{g}
to be simply
\beta_{g}^{U}
. The translation invariance was true by construction. We now only have to find an
\alpha\in\textrm{QAut}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}}}\right)
that satisfies
\omega=\omega_{0}\circ\alpha
. We define
\alpha
as the infinite product automorphism of
\tilde{\alpha}_{L}^{-1}\otimes\tilde{\alpha}_{R}^{-1}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b^{\dagger}}}\right)
. This Automorphism indeed satisfies that
\omega_{0}\vcentcolon=\omega\circ\alpha^{-1}
is a product state. This is because for any
A\in\mathcal{A}_{L_{i}}
,
B\in\mathcal{A}_{L_{j}}
with
i\neq j
we have that
\omega_{0}(A\otimes B)=\phi_{0}(A)\phi_{0}(B)
. We will now show that
\alpha\in\textrm{QAut}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}}}\right)
. To do this, take
0<\theta<\pi/2
and let
b_{j}
be a summable sequence converging to
b
such that
b_{j}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{L_{0}^{n(j)}})
where
n(j)\in\mathbb{N}
is the largest number such that
L_{j}^{n(j)}\subset W(C_{\theta})^{c}
. Define
\Theta\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}}}}\right)
through
\Theta=\bigotimes_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b_{\absolutevalue{j}}^{\dagger}}}\right)_{(\cdot,j)}
. Let
\alpha_{\sigma}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma}}}\right)
be
\alpha_{\sigma}\vcentcolon=\bigotimes_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}(\tilde{\alpha}_{\sigma}^{-1})_{(\cdot,j)}
. Now define
\tilde{V}_{1,m}\vcentcolon=\bigotimes_{j\in\mathbb{Z}\cap[-m,m]}\tau^{j}(b^{\dagger}b_{\absolutevalue{j}})
. We will now show that the limit
\tilde{V}_{1}\vcentcolon=\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{V}_{1,m}
exists (is an element of
\mathcal{A}
). By construction we have that for any
\epsilon
there exists an
n_{0}>0
such that
\epsilon_{n}\vcentcolon=\sum_{i=n}^{\infty}\norm{b-b_i}<\epsilon
for all
n>n_{0}
. We will now show that our sequence is a Cauchy sequence. Let
\epsilon>0
and take
n_{0}
accordingly. For any
n,m\geq n_{0}
we have that

\displaystyle\norm{\tilde{V}_{1,n}-\tilde{V}_{1,m}}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\tilde{V}_{1,n}-\tilde{V}_{1,n_0}}+\norm{\tilde{V}_{1,m}-\tilde{V}_{1,n_0}}=\norm{\tilde{V}_{1,n}\tilde{V}_{1,n_0}^\dagger-\id}+\norm{\tilde{V}_{1,m}\tilde{V}_{1,n_0}^\dagger-\id}.

(8.3)

We will find a bound on the first term as the bound of the second term is analogous. First notice that we have for any tensor product that

A\otimes B-C\otimes D=(A-C+C)\otimes B-C\otimes D=(A-C)\otimes B+C\otimes(B-D).

(8.4)

By using this property and the triangle inequality recursively we get

\displaystyle\norm{\bigotimes_{i=-n}^{-n_0}(b^\dagger b_\abs{i})_i\bigotimes_{i=n_0}^{n}(b^\dagger b_i)_i-\id_{\HH_{L_{-n,\ldots,-n_0}}}\otimes\id_{\HH_{L_{n_0,\ldots,n}}}}\leq 2\sum_{i=n_{0}}^{n}\norm{b^\dagger b_i-\id_{\HH_{L_i}}}<2\epsilon.

(8.5)

This proves that the sequence is a Cauchy sequence and hence the convergence follows. To conclude the proof, we merely have to define
V_{1}\vcentcolon=\alpha_{0}(\tilde{V}_{1})
. We then get

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(V_{1}}}\right)\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta=\alpha_{0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{V}_{1}}}\right)\circ=\bigotimes_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\alpha}_{0}^{-1}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b^{\dagger}b_{\absolutevalue{j}}}}\right)\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b_{\absolutevalue{j}}^{\dagger}}}\right)_{(\cdot,j)}=\bigotimes_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\alpha}_{0}^{-1}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(b^{\dagger}}}\right)_{(\cdot,j)}=\alpha

(8.6)

concluding the proof.
∎

This shows that
\omega
has a well-defined 2D translation index. We will now show that
\textrm{Index}(\omega)=\textrm{Index}_{1d}(\phi)
.

Remark 8.5
.

Remark
8.3
implies that if we take any
k^{R}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},R})
such that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(k^{R}(g)}}\right)\circ\pi_{L_{-1},R}\circ\alpha_{R}|_{L_{-1}}=\pi_{L_{-1},R}\circ\alpha_{R}|_{L_{-1}}\circ\beta_{g}^{L_{-1}\cap R}

(8.7)

then there exists a 2 cochain
C
satisfying that
k^{R}(g)k^{R}(h)k^{R}(gh)^{-1}=C(g,h)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},R}}
. Furthermore, the equivalence class this cochain is in, is given by
\textrm{Index}_{1d}(\phi)
.

Let
\tilde{k}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1}})
be the unique operator that satisfies

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{k}_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{L_{-1}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{L_{-1}}\circ\circ\alpha|_{L_{-1}}\circ\beta_{g}^{L_{-1}}\circ\alpha^{-1}|_{L_{-1}}

\displaystyle\tilde{k}_{g}\Omega_{L_{-1}}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{L_{-1}}.

(8.8)

Similarly, let
\tilde{w}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{L_{>-1},L}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{L_{>-1},R})
be the unique operator such that

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{w}_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{L_{>-1}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{L_{>-1}}\circ\alpha|_{L_{>-1}}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\alpha^{-1}|_{L_{>-1}}

\displaystyle\tilde{w}_{g}\Omega_{L_{>-1}}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{L_{>-1}}.

(8.9)

Clearly, both functions are (linear) representations of the group. Now define

W_{g}=\pi_{0}(V_{1})^{\dagger}(\tilde{w}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{-1}}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}})\pi_{0}(V_{1}).

(8.10)

This operator-valued function is also a linear representation and satisfies the conditions indicated in lemma
5.7
. Since
W_{g}
is a linear representation we get that
W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{-1}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{0}}
. This implies that we can take
u_{\sigma}(g,h)=\bigotimes_{\rho\in\{L_{>-1},L_{-1},L_{<-1}\}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma,\rho}}
(for any
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
). All that is now left is to calculate
K^{\sigma}_{g}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{\sigma})
. We get that

\displaystyle K_{g}

\displaystyle=v^{\dagger}W_{g}vW_{g}^{\dagger}=v^{\dagger}\pi_{0}(V_{1})^{\dagger}(\tilde{w}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1}}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}})\pi_{0}(V_{1})vW_{g}^{\dagger}

(8.11)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}(V_{1})^{\dagger}\tilde{v}^{\dagger}(\tilde{w}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1}}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}})\tilde{v}\pi_{0}(V_{1})W_{g}^{\dagger}.

(8.12)

Notice however that
\tilde{v}^{\dagger}(\tilde{w}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1}}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}})\tilde{v}
and
\tilde{w}_{g}\otimes\tilde{k}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}}
both leave the cyclic vector invariant and have the same adjoint action on the GNS representation. By the uniqueness of such operators, we get that

K_{g}=\pi_{0}(V_{1})^{\dagger}(\tilde{w}_{g}\otimes\tilde{k}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}})\pi_{0}(V_{1})W_{g}^{\dagger}.

(8.13)

Using the adjoint of equation (
8.10
) this gives

\displaystyle K_{g}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}(V_{1})^{\dagger}(\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes\tilde{k}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}})\pi_{0}(V_{1})=\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes\pi_{L_{-1}}(b)^{\dagger}\tilde{k}_{g}\pi_{L_{-1}}(b)\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}}

(8.14)

where
\tilde{v}=\pi_{0}(V_{1})^{\dagger}v\pi_{0}(V_{1})
. Now there exists a
k^{L}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},L})
and a
k^{R}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},R})
such that

k^{L}_{g}\otimes k^{R}_{g}=\pi_{L_{-1}}(b)^{\dagger}\tilde{k}_{g}\pi_{L_{-1}}(b)

(8.15)

and these
k^{\sigma}_{g}
will satisfy what was written in remark
8.3
. Define
K^{\sigma}_{g}=k^{\sigma}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{>-1},\sigma}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1},\sigma}}
. Notice that we have (by construction) that
\forall g,h\in G
,
[K^{\sigma}_{g}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{Z}^{2}/\sigma}},W_{h}]=0
. We now get (using this equation and the fact that
W_{g}
is a representation)

\displaystyle K_{g}^{R}W_{g}K_{h}^{R}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}(K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}\text{\sout{$W_{gh}W_{h}^{\dagger}W_{g}^{\dagger}u_{R}(g,h)$}}=\text{\sout{$W_{g}W_{h}W_{gh}^{\dagger}$}}K_{g}^{R}K_{h}^{R}(K_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}

(8.16)

\displaystyle=k_{g}^{R}k_{h}^{R}(k_{gh}^{R})^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{>-1},\sigma}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1},\sigma}}

and by remark
8.3
this gives that
C(g,h)=\tilde{C}(g,h)
.

8.2
Example with non-trivial
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued index

This example will be very similar to what was done in subsection
8.1
. In this case, however, we will require that the translation morphism
\nu
acts as an automorphism on the
C^{*}
algebra
\mathcal{A}_{L_{i}}
(for all
i
) and that our one-dimensional state is invariant under this automorphism. In this part, we let
\phi\in\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{A})
satisfy assumption
3.6
. Since this state satisfies the split property (see lemma
8.1
) we can define an
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
-valued index for it (see section
4
for a construction of this index). Now we will take again the infinite tensor product (see definition
8.2
) of such states then we get:

Lemma 8.6
.

Let
\omega_{\phi}
be the infinite tensor product of a state
\phi
that satisfies assumption
3.6
then
\omega_{\phi}
satisfies assumption
3.5
.

Proof.

This lemma follows from lemma
8.4
.
∎

This shows that
\omega_{\phi}
indeed has a well-defined
H^{1}(G,\mathbb{T})
index. We will now show that
\textrm{Index}_{\text{2 trans}}(\omega_{\phi})=\textrm{Index}_{\text{1d trans}}(\phi)
.

Remark 8.7
.

Remark
8.3
implies that if we take any
k^{R}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},R}
such that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(k^{R}(g)}}\right)\circ\pi_{L_{-1},R}\circ\alpha_{R}|_{L_{-1}}=\pi_{L_{-1},R}\circ\alpha_{R}|_{L_{-1}}\circ\beta_{g}^{L_{-1}\cap R}

(8.17)

and
w_{L_{-1}}
to be such that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w_{L_{-1}}}}\right)\circ\pi_{L_{-1},L}\otimes\pi_{L_{-1},R}=\pi_{L_{-1},L}\otimes\pi_{L_{-1},R}\circ\nu

(8.18)

then there exists a
U(1)
representation
\alpha
satisfying that

(\pi_{L_{-1},L}(U_{(-1,-1)}(g))\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},R}})(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},L}}\otimes k^{R}(g))=\alpha(g)w_{L_{-1}}^{\dagger}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},L}}\otimes k^{R}(g))w_{L_{-1}}

(8.19)

and that it is given by
\textrm{Index}_{\text{1d trans}}(\phi)
.

Just like in subsection
8.1
, let
\tilde{k}:G\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1}})
be the unique operator that satisfies

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{k}_{g}}}\right)\circ\pi_{L_{-1}}

\displaystyle=\pi_{L_{-1}}\circ\circ\alpha|_{L_{-1}}\circ\beta_{g}^{L_{-1}}\circ\alpha^{-1}|_{L_{-1}}

\displaystyle\tilde{k}_{g}\Omega_{L_{-1}}

\displaystyle=\Omega_{L_{-1}}.

(8.20)

We can take

K_{g}=\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes\pi_{L_{-1}}(b)^{\dagger}\tilde{k}_{g}\pi_{L_{-1}}(b)\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}}=\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes(k_{g}^{L}\otimes k_{g}^{R})\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{<-1}}}.

(8.21)

Similarly we can take
w_{\rho}\in\mathcal{H}_{\rho}
(where
\rho\in\{L_{>-1},L_{-1},L_{<-1}\}
) satisfying

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w_{\rho}}}\right)\circ\pi_{\rho}\circ\alpha_{0}|_{\rho}\circ\Theta|_{\rho}=\pi_{\rho}\circ\alpha_{0}|_{\rho}\circ\Theta|_{\rho}\circ\nu

(8.22)

and then we get that
w=w_{L_{>-1}}\otimes w_{L_{-1}}\otimes w_{L_{<-1}}
. Since
\eta_{g}=\textrm{Id}_{\mathcal{A}}
we can simply take
b_{g}^{\sigma}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{\sigma}}
. We now have that

\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(U_{(-1,-1)})\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}\otimes K_{g}^{R}=\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes(\pi_{L_{-1},L}\circ\tilde{\alpha}_{L}(U_{(-1,-1)})\otimes k^{R}(g))\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{>-1}}}.

(8.23)

By what was written in remark
8.7
we get that

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes\alpha(g)w_{L_{-1}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},L}}\otimes k^{R}(g))w_{L_{-1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{<-1}}}

(8.24)

\displaystyle=\alpha(g)w^{\dagger}(\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{>-1}}}\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L_{-1},L}}\otimes k^{R}(g)\otimes\mathbbm{1}_{H_{L_{<-1}}})w=\alpha(g)w^{\dagger}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{H}_{L}}\otimes K^{R}_{g})w

showing that indeed
\textrm{Index}_{\text{2 trans}}(\omega_{\phi})=\textrm{Index}_{\text{1d trans}}(\phi)
.

9
Invariance of the indices under LGAs

The goal of this section is now to show that the indices we constructed are invariant under locally generated automorphisms. More specifically, if we let
H\in\mathcal{B}_{F_{\phi}}([0,1])
, be a
G
-invariant, translation invariant (in the vertical direction) one-parameter family of interactions, then we will show that
\textrm{Index}(\omega\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1})=\textrm{Index}(\omega)
. If additionally,
\omega
and
H
are translation invariant in both directions then we will show that
\textrm{Index}_{\text{2 trans}}(\omega\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1})=\textrm{Index}_{\text{2 trans}}(\omega)
.

9.1
The morphisms
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}

The goal of this section is to define a pair of morphisms,
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
, that will be used for defining the operators belonging to
\omega\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}
. For notational simplicity in this section
{H_{\text{split}}}=H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}+H_{\nu(R)}
(with
H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}
and
H_{\nu(R)}
the restrictions of
H
as defined in
2.3
). Clearly we have that
\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{t;s}=\gamma^{H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{\nu(R)}}_{t;s}
and we still have that
\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{t;s}\circ\tau=\tau\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{t;s}
(just like we have for
H
). Additionally, in the case where there are two translation symmetries, we get that
\nu({H_{\text{split}}})=H_{L}+H_{\nu\circ\nu(R)}
while
\nu^{-1}({H_{\text{split}}})=H_{\nu^{-1}\circ\nu^{-1}(L)}+H_{R}
.

For the remainder of the section, we will use a decomposition using the following lemma:

Lemma 9.1
.

Take
0<\theta<\pi/2
and
\Theta\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}}}}\right)
. There exist
\Phi^{U}_{0;1}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U}}}\right)
and
\Phi^{D}_{0;1}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D}}}\right)
, both commuting with
\beta_{g}
, such that there exists some
a\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A})
satisfying that

\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\Phi^{U}_{0;1}\otimes\Phi^{D}_{0;1}.

(9.1)

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\in\textrm{GVAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A})
(see lemma
B.3
part 2 which was based on similar statements made in
[
10
]
).
∎

In what follows, we will define
\tilde{a}\vcentcolon=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(a)
. The main property we will use of this operator is that it satisfies the following lemma:

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
A\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})\cap\sigma})
then

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}(A)=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A).

(9.2)

Proof.

Using lemma
9.1
we get that

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)(A)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\Phi^{U}_{1;0}\otimes\Phi^{D}_{1;0}(A)

(9.3)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A)

here
\Phi_{1;0}=\Phi_{0;1}^{-1}
. This concludes the proof.
∎

We will then use this property to define a morphism:

Lemma 9.3
.

Let
\tilde{a}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(a)
and define the map

\displaystyle\phi_{1,\sigma}

\displaystyle:\textrm{IAC}_{\sigma}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}):x=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A)y\mapsto\phi_{1}(x)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A)\tilde{a}y\tilde{a}^{\dagger}

(9.4)

where
A\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma})
and
y\in\textrm{Cone}_{\sigma}(\alpha_{0},\theta)
. This map satisfies that

1.

for (the unique)
\xi\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})\cap\sigma}}}\right)
, such that
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\xi
, we get that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{1,\sigma}(x)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A}}\right)\circ\xi.

(9.5)

2.

it is well-defined (independent of the choice of
A
and
y
).

3.

it is a group homomorphism.

4.

\tilde{a}^{\dagger}\phi_{1}(x)\tilde{a}\in\textrm{IAC}_{\sigma}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta\circ\Phi^{U}_{0;1}\otimes\Phi^{D}_{0;1})
.

5.

if additionally,
x\in\textrm{IAC}_{\sigma}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
,
\textrm{IAC}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
or
\textrm{IAC}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
then
\tilde{a}^{\dagger}\phi_{1}(x)\tilde{a}\in\textrm{IAC}_{\sigma}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta\circ\Phi^{U}_{0;1}\otimes\Phi^{D}_{0;1})
,
\textrm{IAC}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta\circ\Phi^{U}_{0;1}\otimes\Phi^{D}_{0;1})
or
\textrm{IAC}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)}^{W}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta\circ\Phi^{U}_{0;1}\otimes\Phi^{D}_{0;1})
respectively.

Proof.

In this proof, we take
\sigma=R
, the proof when
\sigma=L
is equivalent. Take
x\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
arbitrary. Take
A\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{R})
and
y\in\textrm{Cone}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
such that
x=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A)y
and take
\xi\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})}\cap\mathcal{A}_{R}}}\right)
the automorphism such that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\xi.

(9.6)

Take
a
and
\Phi=\Phi^{U}\otimes\Phi^{D}
as given in equation (
9.1
). We now define

\phi_{1,R}(x)\vcentcolon=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A)\tilde{a}y\tilde{a}^{\dagger}.

(9.7)

We now have to prove three things:

1.

It satisfies equation (
9.5
).

2.

This map is well-defined (independent of our choices).

3.

This is a group homomorphism.

To show the first item just observe that

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{1,R}(x)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A)\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(a)y\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(a)^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}

(9.8)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\xi\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}.

Now we insert
\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{H}_{1;0}=\text{Id}
and obtain:

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ(\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{H}_{1;0})

(9.9)

\displaystyle\quad\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\xi\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A}}\right)\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{H}_{1;0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\xi\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}.

(9.10)

Using equation (
9.1
) this gives

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A}}\right)\circ\Phi^{-1}\circ\xi\circ\Phi=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A}}\right)\circ\xi

(9.11)

concluding the proof of the first item. To show the second item, suppose we have two different representatives of
x
, say

x=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{1})y_{1}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{2})y_{2}.

(9.12)

By construction, this implies that
\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{2}^{\dagger}A_{1})=y_{2}y_{1}^{\dagger}
. This in turn implies that
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{2}^{\dagger}A_{2}}}\right)\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})\cap R}}}\right)
and hence we require that
A_{2}^{\dagger}A_{1}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{(W(C_{\theta})\cap R)^{c}}^{\prime})=\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})\cap R})
(this last equality follows from section 3 of
[
7
]
). By using this equation combined with lemma
9.2
we get that

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}}}\right)(y_{2}y_{1}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{2}^{\dagger}A_{1})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{2}^{\dagger}A_{1}).

(9.13)

Reworking this equation gives the desired

\phi_{1,R}(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{1})y_{1})=\phi_{1,R}(\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{2})y_{2}).

(9.14)

To then show the third item, take
x_{1},x_{2}\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\Theta)
arbitrary. Take
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{R}),y_{1},y_{2}\in\textrm{Cone}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\Theta)
and
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})}\cap\mathcal{A}_{R}}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle x_{i}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{i})y_{i}

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(y_{i}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\xi_{i}.

(9.15)

We have

\displaystyle x_{1}x_{2}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{1})y_{1}\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{2})y_{2}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{1})y_{1}\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{2})y_{1}^{\dagger}y_{1}y_{2}

(9.16)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A_{1}\xi_{1}(A_{2}))y_{1}y_{2}

giving

\phi_{1,R}(x_{1}x_{2})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{1}\xi_{1}(A_{2}))\tilde{a}y_{1}y_{2}\tilde{a}^{\dagger}.

(9.17)

where
\tilde{a}=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(a)
. Filling this in gives

\displaystyle\phi_{1,R}(x_{1}x_{2})\phi_{1,R}(x_{2})^{\dagger}\phi_{1,R}(x_{1})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{1}\xi_{1}(A_{2}))\tilde{a}y_{1}\text{\sout{$y_{2}\tilde{a}^{\dagger}\tilde{a}y_{2}^{\dagger}$}}\tilde{a}^{\dagger}

(9.18)

\displaystyle\quad\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{2}^{\dagger})\tilde{a}y_{1}^{\dagger}\tilde{a}^{\dagger}\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{1}^{\dagger}).

Writing out a part of this gives:

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}y_{1}\tilde{a}^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{2}^{\dagger})

(9.19)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\xi_{1}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{2}^{\dagger}).

Using equation (
9.1
) now yields:

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\xi_{1}\circ\Phi(A_{2}^{\dagger})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\Phi\circ\xi_{1}(A_{2}^{\dagger})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\xi_{1}(A_{2}^{\dagger}).

(9.20)

This shows that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}y_{1}\tilde{a}^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}(A_{2}^{\dagger})=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\xi_{1}(A_{2}^{\dagger}).

(9.21)

Inserting this in equation (
9.18
) shows that
\phi_{1,R}(x_{1}x_{2})\phi_{1,R}(x_{1})^{\dagger}\phi_{1,R}(x_{2})^{\dagger}=\mathbbm{1}
, concluding the proof of the third item. To show the last two items one only has to work out
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}^{\dagger}\phi_{1,R}(x)\tilde{a}}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta
, in both situations. Doing this concludes the proof.
∎

We now have to extend the above definition to the group

\textrm{IAC}_{L\times R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)=\{ab=ba|a\in\textrm{IAC}_{L}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta),b\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)\}.

(9.22)

Lemma 9.4
.

Define

\phi_{1}:\textrm{IAC}_{L\times R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}):ab\mapsto\phi_{1,L}(a)\phi_{1,R}(b)

(9.23)

where
a\in\textrm{IAC}_{L}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
and
b\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
. This satisfies two points.

1.

It is well-defined, by which we mean that for any
a,\tilde{a}\in\textrm{IAC}_{L}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
and for any
b,\tilde{b}\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
such that
ab=\tilde{a}\tilde{b}
we have that
\phi_{1,L}(a)\phi_{1,R}(b)=\phi_{1,L}(\tilde{a})\phi_{1,R}(\tilde{b})
.

2.

It is still a group homomorphism.

Proof.

The first item is trivial, this is because the fact that
ab=\tilde{a}\tilde{b}
already implies that
(a,b)
and
(\tilde{a},\tilde{b})
can only differ up to an exchange in phase. To show the second item, we only have to show that
\phi_{1,L}(a)\phi_{1,R}(b)=\phi_{1,R}(b)\phi_{1,L}(a)
for any
a\in\textrm{IAC}_{L}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
and for any
b\in\textrm{IAC}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
. To show this, we first write out
b=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(B)y_{b}
where
B\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{R})
and where
y_{b}\in\textrm{Cone}_{R}(\alpha_{0},\theta)
. We can now split the proof into two parts. First, we want to prove that

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{1,L}(a)}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma_{0;1}^{H}\circ\gamma^{H_{\text{split}}}_{1;0}(B)=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma_{0;1}^{H}\circ\gamma^{H_{\text{split}}}_{1;0}(B).

(9.24)

This can be done using equation (
9.5
). Secondly, we want to prove that
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{1,L}(a)}}\right)(\phi_{1,R}(y_{b}))=(\phi_{1,R}(y_{b}))
. We can rewrite this equation to

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{1,R}(y_{b})}}\right)(\phi_{1,L}(a))=(\phi_{1,L}(a)).

(9.25)

We now write out
a=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta(A)y_{a}
with
A\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{L})
and
y_{a}\in\textrm{Cone}_{L}(\alpha_{0},\theta)
. We again split the proof of this equality into two parts. The equality with the invariance of the
A
part again uses equation (
9.5
). The equality with the invariance of the
y_{a}
part can be done by inserting the definition and observing that the
\tilde{a}
cancels.
∎

Lemma 9.5
.

Define again
\tilde{a}
through equation (
9.1
). Let

\phi_{2}:\{\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{-p}}}\right)(W_{g})|g\in G,p\in\{-1,0,1\}\}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H}):\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v^{-p}}}\right)(W_{g})\mapsto\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}v^{-p}}}\right)(W_{g})

(9.26)

for the case where there is one translation symmetry, let

\displaystyle\phi_{2}

\displaystyle:\{\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{-s}v^{-p}}}\right)(W_{g})|g\in G\text{ and }s,p\in\{-1,0,1\}\}\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{H})

(9.27)

\displaystyle:\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{-s}v^{-p}}}\right)(W_{g})\mapsto\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\tilde{a}w^{-s}v^{-p}}}\right)(W_{g})

in the case where
\omega
and
H
are translation invariant in both directions. This satisfies

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{2}(w^{-s}v^{-p}W_{g}v^{p}w^{s})}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

(9.28)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{1;0}\circ\nu^{-s}\circ\tau^{-p}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\tau^{p}\circ\nu^{s}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{H}_{1;0}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

(where
s=0
in the case where there is just one translation symmetry).

Proof.

To show that this indeed satisfies equation (
9.28
) observe that using equation (
9.1
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\phi_{2}(w^{-s}v^{-p}W_{g}v^{p}w^{s})}}\right)\circ\pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\nu^{-s}\circ\tau^{-p}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\tau^{p}\circ\nu^{s}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a^{\dagger}}}\right)\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}

(9.29)

\displaystyle=\pi_{0}\circ\alpha_{0}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{H_{L}}_{1;0}\otimes\gamma^{H_{L}}_{1;0}\circ\text{\sout{$\Phi_{0;1}^{-1}$}}\circ\nu^{-s}\circ\tau^{-p}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\tau^{p}\circ\nu^{s}\circ\beta_{g}^{\tau^{-p}(U)}\circ\text{\sout{$\Phi_{0;1}$}}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{0;1}\circ\gamma^{H}_{1;0}\circ\Theta^{-1}\circ\alpha_{0}^{-1}.

Since
\Phi
commutes with both the
\nu^{-s}\circ\tau^{-p}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\tau^{p}\circ\nu^{s}
and with the
\beta_{g}^{\tau^{-p}(U)}
the result follows.
∎

Lemma 9.6
.

Take
\phi_{1}
and
\phi_{2}
as defined previously. Take
s\in\{-1,0,1\}
and take
p\in\{-1,0,1\}
arbitrary in the case where
\omega
and
H
have two translation symmetries while we take
p=0
in the case where we only have one translation symmetry. For any
x\in\textrm{IAC}_{L\times R}(\alpha_{0},\theta,\Theta)
, we have that

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(w^{-p}W_{g}w^{p}}}\right)(x))
