X\cap C_{1}\neq\emptyset,X\cap C_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}(\norm{\Psi^{(1)}(X;t)})\absolutevalue{X}G_{F}(m)}}\right)

(B.6)

is now defined using the new
\mathcal{C}_{1}
. To bound these
M(C_{1},C_{2})
we will (just like in
[
10
]
) differentiate between two cases. That is, the case where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are adjacent
21
21

21

We say that
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are adjacent if and only if
\#(C_{1}(1)\cap C_{2}(1))=\infty
.
and the case where they are not. We begin with the latter. In this case, we still have in complete analogy with the proof in
[
10
]
that

\displaystyle M(C_{1},C_{2})\leq b_{0}(C_{1},C_{2})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\sum_{m\geq 0}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:X\cap C_{1}\neq\emptyset,\\
X\cap C_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sup_{t\in[0,1]}(\norm{\Psi^{(1)}(X;t)})\absolutevalue{X}G_{F}(m)}}\right)

(B.7)

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\Psi_1}_{F}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in C_{1}\\
y\in C_{2}\end{subarray}}F(d(x,y))\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}G_{F}(m)<\infty.

(B.8)

22
22

22

Here
\Psi_{1}
is defined through
\Psi_{1}(X)=\absolutevalue{X}^{1}\Psi(X)
.

What is now left to show is the case where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are adjacent. Take
\tilde{C}\in\mathcal{C}_{1}
such that
C_{1}\cap\tilde{C}\neq\emptyset
and
C_{2}\cap\tilde{C}\neq\emptyset
. Take
L_{1}=\partial\tilde{C}/C_{2}
23
23

23

Here
\partial\tilde{C}
means the boundary of
\tilde{C}
.
and
L_{2}=\partial\tilde{C}/C_{1}
. By following the same reasoning that led to equation (5.36) in
[
10
]
we get that

\displaystyle M(C_{1},C_{2})\leq b_{0}(C_{1},C_{2}/\tilde{C})+b_{0}(C_{1}/\tilde{C},C_{2})+b_{0}(\tilde{C}\cap C_{2},(C_{1}\cup C_{2})^{c})+b_{1}(C_{1}\cap\tilde{C},C_{2}\cap\tilde{C},L_{1},L_{2})

(B.9)

where

\displaystyle b_{1}(C_{1}\cap\tilde{C},C_{2}\cap\tilde{C},L_{1},L_{2})

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\subset\tilde{C}:\\
X\cap C_{1}\cap\tilde{C}\neq\emptyset\\
X\cap C_{2}\cap\tilde{C}\neq\emptyset\\
X\cap(\tilde{C}^{c}(m))\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\norm{\Psi(X;t)}\absolutevalue{X}G_{F}(m)

(B.10)

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\Psi_1}_{F}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}G_{F}(m)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in C_{2}\cap\tilde{C},\\
y\in L_{1}(m)\end{subarray}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in C_{1}\cap\tilde{C},\\
y\in L_{2}(m)\end{subarray}}}}\right)F(d(x,y))<\infty.

(B.11)

Since the remainder of the proof can remain unchanged from
[
10
]
, this concludes the proof of item 3. The proof of item 5 just follows from the fact that for any
\alpha_{1}\in\textrm{SQAut}(\mathcal{A})
and for any
\alpha_{2}\in\textrm{HAut}(\mathcal{A})
we have that
\alpha_{2}\circ\alpha_{1}\in\textrm{SQAut}(\mathcal{A})
. We now only need to comment on item 2. We must show that

(\gamma^{H}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H_{L}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{R}}_{t;s})^{-1}=\gamma^{H_{L}}_{s;t}\otimes\gamma^{H_{R}}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\in\textrm{GVAut}(\mathcal{A}).

(B.12)

The proof of this starts analogously to the proof of items 1 and 3. We take
\Psi=H
,
\Psi^{(0)}=H_{\tau(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap U)}+H_{\tau^{-1}(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap D)}
and
\Psi^{(1)}=\Psi-\Psi^{(0)}
. Define
\Xi^{(s)}(Z;t)
again through equation (
B.3
). In analogy to what was done in equation (5.54) in
[
10
]
we obtain

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z:Z\nsubseteq\tau(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap U)\\
\text{and }Z\nsubseteq\tau^{-1}(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap D)\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\norm{\Xi^{(1)}(Z,t)}\leq\frac{8}{C_{F}}(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1)\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X:X(m)\nsubseteq\tau(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap U)\\
\text{and }X(m)\nsubseteq\tau^{-1}(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap D)\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\norm{\Psi^{(1)}(X;t)}\absolutevalue{X}G_{F}(m).

(B.13)

If
X
in the last line has a non-zero contribution, then at least one of the following occurs:

1.

X\cap(W(C_{\theta})\cap L)\neq\emptyset
and
X\cap R\neq\emptyset
.

2.

X\cap(W(C_{\theta})\cap R)\neq\emptyset
and
X\cap L\neq\emptyset
.

3.

X\subset W(C_{\theta})^{c}
and

(a)

X\subset U,X\subset D
, or

(b)

X\subset U,X\subset L,X\subset R
and
X(m)\cap(\tau^{-1}(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap D))^{c}\neq\emptyset
, or

(c)

X\subset D,X\subset L,X\subset R
and
X(m)\cap(\tau(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap U))^{c}\neq\emptyset
.

This shows that we have a bound

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Z:Z\nsubseteq\tau(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap U)\\
\text{and }Z\nsubseteq\tau^{-1}(C_{\theta}^{c}\cap D)\end{subarray}}\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\norm{\Xi^{(1)}(Z,t)}

(B.14)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{C_{F}}(e^{2I_{F}(\Psi)}-1)(b_{0}(W(C_{\theta})\cap L,R)+b_{0}(L,W(C_{\theta})\cap R)+b_{0}(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U,W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D)

\displaystyle\qquad+b_{1}(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U\cap L,W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U\cap L,L\cap\partial(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U),R\cap\partial(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U))

\displaystyle\qquad b_{1}(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D\cap L,W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D\cap R,L\cap\partial(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D),R\cap\partial(W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D)))<\infty.

(B.15)

This concludes the proof.
∎

This implies certain things for our locally generated automorphisms. From these four statements we can prove the following results:

Lemma B.4
.

Take
H
an interaction such that there exists a
0<\phi<1
satisfying that
\norm{H}_{f_{\phi}}\leq 1
. Take
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
such that
0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}<\pi/2
then for all
\Theta\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta_{2}})^{c}}}}\right)
and
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
there exists an
a_{1}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A})
and a
\tilde{\Theta}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta_{1}})^{c}}}}\right)
such that

\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{1}}}\right)\circ\tilde{\Theta}.

(B.16)

Proof.

We have that

\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}=\gamma^{H_{D}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{s;t}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{s;t}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{s;t}.

(B.17)

Using that
\gamma^{H}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{t;s}\in\textrm{HAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A})
we get that there exists some
a\in\mathcal{A}
and
\eta\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{C_{\theta}}}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{s;t}^{-1}\circ\Theta\circ\eta_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{s;t}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{s;t}

(B.18)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a}}\right)\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{t;s}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{t;s}\circ\Theta\circ\gamma^{H_{D}}_{s;t}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{s;t}.

(B.19)

Since by
B.3
part 3
\gamma^{H_{D}}_{s;t}\otimes\gamma^{H_{U}}_{s;t}\in\textrm{GSQAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A})
the result follows.
∎

Lemma B.5
.

Take
H
an interaction such that there exists a
0<\phi<1
satisfying that
\norm{H}_{f_{\phi}}\leq 1
. Take
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
such that
0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}<\pi/2
. Then for all
\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(C_{\theta_{1}}\cap\sigma)}}}\right)
(where
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
and
g\in G
) and
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
there exist
a_{2},\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}),a_{3,\sigma}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)})
and some
\tilde{\eta}_{\sigma}^{g}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\nu^{\sigma}(\mathcal{A}_{C_{\theta_{2}}\cap\sigma)}}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{2}}}\right)\circ(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{R})

(B.20)

\displaystyle\gamma^{H_{\sigma}}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\circ\gamma^{H_{\sigma}}_{s;t}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{3,\sigma}}}\right)\circ\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}.

(B.21)

Proof.

In this proof, take
{H_{\text{split}}}=H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}+H_{\nu(R)}
. First, we show that equation (
B.21
) implies equation (
B.20
). This is because using equation (
B.20
) we get that

\displaystyle\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}

(B.22)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{s;t}\circ\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{3,L}\otimes a_{3,R}}}\right)\circ\tilde{\eta}_{g}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}.

(B.23)

If one now uses the fact that
\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{{H_{\text{split}}}}_{s;t}\in\textrm{GVAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A})
(see lemma
B.3
item 2) the implication follows. To finish the proof we now only have to use the fact that the
\gamma^{H_{\sigma}}_{0;1}
are in
\textrm{SQAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma})
.
∎

Additionally, when we add the group action, we get:

Lemma B.6
.

Take
H
a
G
-invariant interaction such that there exists a
0<\phi<1
satisfying that
\norm{H}_{f_{\phi}}\leq 1
. Take
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
such that
0<\theta_{1}<\theta_{2}<\pi/2
. Then for all
\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(C_{\theta_{1}}\cap\sigma)}}}\right)
(where
\sigma\in\{L,R\}
and
g\in G
) and
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
there exist
a_{2},\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}),a_{3,\sigma}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)})
and some
\tilde{\eta}_{\sigma}^{g}\in\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\nu^{\sigma}(\mathcal{A}_{C_{\theta_{2}}\cap\sigma)}}}\right)
such that

\displaystyle\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}^{L}\otimes\eta_{g}^{R}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{2}}}\right)\circ(\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{L}\otimes\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{R})\circ\beta_{g}^{U}

(B.24)

\displaystyle\gamma^{H_{\sigma}}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}^{\sigma}\circ\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}\circ\gamma^{H_{\sigma}}_{s;t}

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{3,\sigma}}}\right)\circ\tilde{\eta}_{g}^{\sigma}\circ\beta_{g}^{\sigma U}.

(B.25)

Proof.

In this proof, take again
{H_{\text{split}}}=H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}+H_{\nu(R)}
. First, we show that equation (
B.25
) implies equation (
B.24
). This is because using (the inverse of) equation (
B.24
) we get that

\displaystyle\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}\circ\beta_{g^{-1}}^{U}\circ(\tilde{\eta}_{g})^{-1}

(B.26)

\displaystyle=(\text{Inner})\circ\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H_{\text{split}}}_{t;s}\circ\beta_{g^{-1}}^{U}\circ(\eta_{g})^{-1}\circ\gamma^{H_{\text{split}}}_{s;t}\circ\underline{\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}}.

(B.27)

Now using the fact that by lemma
B.3
item 2,
\gamma^{H}_{s;t}\circ\gamma^{H_{\text{split}}}_{t;s}\in\textrm{GVAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A})
this gives us

\displaystyle=(\text{Inner})\circ\text{\sout{$\gamma^{H}_{t;s}\circ\eta_{g}\circ\beta_{g}^{U}\circ\beta_{g^{-1}}^{U}\circ(\eta_{g})^{-1}\circ\gamma^{H}_{s;t}$}}.

(B.28)

By taking
a_{2}
such that
\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(a_{2}}}\right)
is this inner automorphism, we can conclude the proof that equation (
B.25
) implies equation (
B.24
). Now we only have to prove equation (
B.25
). To finish the proof we now only have to use the fact that the
\gamma^{H_{\nu^{\sigma}(\sigma)}}_{0;1}
are in
\textrm{GSQAut}_{1}(\mathcal{A}_{\sigma})
.
∎

Lemma B.7
.

There exist maps

\displaystyle A_{L}

\displaystyle:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{L}):\lambda\mapsto A_{L}(\lambda)

\displaystyle A_{R}

\displaystyle:[0,1]\rightarrow\mathcal{U}(\mathcal{A}_{R}):\lambda\mapsto A_{R}(\lambda)

(B.29)

both continuous in norm topology and

\displaystyle\Phi^{\mu\nu}:[0,1]\rightarrow\textrm{Aut}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathcal{A}_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap\mu\cap\nu}}}\right):\lambda\mapsto\Phi^{\mu\nu}(\lambda)

(B.30)

where
\mu\in\{U,D\}
and
\nu\in\{L,\nu(R)\}
, all four continuous in strong
24
24

24

Meaning that
\Phi^{\mu\nu}(\lambda)(A)
is continuous for all
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
topology, satisfying that

\displaystyle\nu\circ\gamma^{{\tilde{H}_{\text{split}}}}_{0;\lambda}\circ\nu^{-1}\circ\gamma^{{\tilde{H}_{\text{split}}}}_{\lambda;0}

\displaystyle=\gamma^{H_{L}}_{0;\lambda}\otimes\gamma^{H_{\nu\circ\nu(R)}}_{0;\lambda}\circ\gamma^{{\tilde{H}_{\text{split}}}}_{\lambda;0}

(B.31)

\displaystyle=\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{L}(\lambda)}}\right)\otimes\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{R}(\lambda)}}\right)\circ\bigotimes_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in\{U,D\},\\
\nu\in\{L,\nu(R)\}\end{subarray}}\Phi^{\mu\nu}(\lambda)

(B.32)

and that
\Phi^{\mu\nu}\circ\beta_{g}=\beta_{g}\circ\Phi^{\mu\nu}
.

Proof.

The proof of this is very similar to all the other proofs in this section. We will go over the highlights. We want to define an
A_{L}(\lambda)
and a
\Phi^{U,L}\otimes\Phi^{D,L}
satisfying

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{L}(\lambda)}}\right)\circ\Phi^{U,L}\otimes\Phi^{D,L}(\lambda)=\gamma^{H_{L}}_{0;\lambda}\circ\gamma^{H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}}_{\lambda;0}

(B.33)

and similarly for the right side. We will only work out this side as the other calculation is analogous. We do this in two steps. First, we find an interaction
\Xi
satisfying

\gamma^{\Xi}_{0;\lambda}=\gamma^{H_{L}}_{0;\lambda}\circ\gamma^{H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}}_{\lambda;0}.

(B.34)

We can do this by taking the usual

\Xi(Z,t)\vcentcolon=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\subset Z\\
X(m)=Z\end{subarray}}\Delta_{X(m)}(\gamma^{H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}}_{0;t}(\tilde{H}(X,t)))

(B.35)

where
\tilde{H}\vcentcolon=H_{L}-H_{\nu^{-1}(L)}
. We can now define

\Phi^{\mu L}(\lambda)\vcentcolon=\gamma^{\Xi_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap\mu\cap L}}_{0;\lambda}.

(B.36)

This means that we can satisfy
B.32
if

\displaystyle\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{L}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{\Xi}_{0;\lambda}\circ\gamma^{\Xi^{\prime}}_{\lambda;0}

\displaystyle\Xi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\Xi_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap U\cap L}+\Xi_{W(C_{\theta})^{c}\cap D\cap L}.

(B.37)

Using the same argument as before, this can be realized when we have

\textrm{Ad}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(A_{L}(\lambda)}}\right)=\gamma^{\tilde{\Xi}}_{0;\lambda}

(B.38)

where

\tilde{\Xi}(Z,t)\vcentcolon=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\subset Z\\
X(m)=Z\end{subarray}}\Delta_{X(m)}(\gamma^{\Xi^{\prime}}_{0;t}(\Xi(X,t)-\Xi^{\prime}(X,t))).

(B.39)

The last part of the proof is to show that
\norm{\sum_{Z\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ZZ^2}}\tilde\Xi(Z,t)}<\infty
which can be done using similar techniques as before. This means that we can simply define

A_{L}(\lambda)\vcentcolon=\mathcal{T}\exp(-i\int_0^\lambda\d s\: \sum_{Z\in\mathfrak{B}_{\ZZ^2}}\tilde{\Xi}(Z,s))

(B.40)

and this is by construction bounded and continuous in
\lambda
.
∎
